# Final stabilization

Continuation of `production-hardening.md`; the demonstration remains stopped.

## Acceptance inventory

- Close additive schema deployment and verify a database backup by restoring into an isolated database and comparing table checksums.
- Record exact historical reconciliation records without reconstructing unknown facts.
- Protect consequential metadata editors against stale writes; verify API, UI, imports and audit behavior together.
- Measure important staff requests before optimization; record repeatable before/after results.
- Verify staff forms, persistence, filters, loading/error states, retries and stale/two-tab operations in the existing headed Chromium session.
- Run complete safe PHP/JS regressions, syntax, permission, concurrency, rollback and import/export checks after final changes.
- Separately inspect visual layout and error recovery. Exploratory cases include rapid filtering and stale form submission.

## Current gates - updated after Houssein's D1-D5 and owner-control decisions

**NOT READY.** Historical evidence and approved numbering remain outstanding; five retained accounts still need recoverable rotation after owner/key/audit activation; A3/A5/A6 remain unmapped, A4 is approved for Cherry with no extra permission required, and account 1 ownership is not yet verified. No off-host destination exists. Backblaze B2 is a proposed destination only. Owner incident and escrow controls are implemented and locally verified; production activation remains disabled.

Manual/approved translations and disabled tracking/email/WhatsApp are approved internal-release scope, not requirements for a live provider. Retention durations and recovery/session objectives are approved below. Earlier inventories are retained as dated evidence, not unanswered repetitions of D1-D5.

## Original blocker inventory (superseded decisions; record IDs remain evidence)

- Active seed-password accounts: 3 (QA China Admin), 4 (QA Warehouse Staff), 5 (QA Lebanon Admin), 6 (QA China Employee), 11 (imad). Decide which accounts to disable and which to retain with securely delivered replacement credentials. Pending user question; unchanged.
- Unallocated active receipts: 28 / order 24, 30 / order 34, 34 / order 23, 211 / order 734, 212 / order 735, 213 / order 736, 214 / order 737, 215 / order 738. Supply item allocations, received quantities and actual measurements from signed warehouse records; decide correction/reversal of finalized orders 734-738.
- Order 28, item 55: received 180 versus ordered 168. Confirm actual quantity and whether the purchase or receipt is incorrect; no invented reversal.
- Buyer 16, duplicate item number `AS-1`: item IDs 68, 94; order IDs 34, 52. Approve canonical numbering and reference remapping before any merge or renumber.
- Buyer 17, duplicate item number `CUARRi`: item IDs 229-743; order IDs 170. Approve canonical numbering and reference remapping before any merge or renumber.
- Missing measurement basis: 634 product-linked order items, IDs 1-63, 68, 87-89, 93-96, 98-103, 121-123, 229-743, 807-810, 1080, 1379-1397, 1428-1442. Confirm historical piece/carton basis against purchase documents; current catalog values are not historical evidence.
- Legacy template items 1–12, in templates 1–6 (two successive item IDs per template): measurement basis remains NULL. Recreate from verified piece/carton measurements before reuse; new templates retain their explicit basis.
- Upload retention: approve age and retention obligations for unattached owned previews; historical files without ownership are excluded from automatic deletion.
- Remote tracking: provide a sandbox endpoint and expected acknowledgement/idempotency contract. No customer tracking is sent.
- Supply operational ownership/retention decisions for immutable off-host audit storage and classification/translation review. Missing historical audit events cannot be reconstructed.
- Classification review: product IDs 1–62, 69–71, 78–83, 146–660, 685–688, 878, 1062–1080, 1096–1110 (625 records); order-item IDs 1–63, 68, 87–89, 93–103, 121, 123, 229–743, 807–810, 1428–1447 (619 records). A responsible operator must confirm goods classification from actual specifications; no inferred historical classification was applied.
- Translation provider/review decision: pending/failed job IDs 1–8, 10–12, 14–18, 21, 23–25, 27–30, 33–38, 41–46, 49–50, 68, 87, 101, 116, 120, 122, 125, 128, 132, 136, 140, 143, 147, 151, 155, 159, 163, 167, 171–182, 184–185 (70 records). Approve provider/credentials and review output quality before enabling external translation.

## Verified deployment/recovery

Database backup at `C:/xampp/backups/clms-stabilization-20260919/before-schema.sql` (3,095,728 bytes), SHA-256 and timestamps in adjacent `restore-evidence.json`. All 68 restored base-table checksums matched source. Migrations 082 and 083 were rehearsed in the restore database, then applied to source; preflight now verifies release migrations, schema and backup rehearsal. This is database recovery evidence, not an off-host or whole-installation disaster-recovery claim.

## Fixes and downstream verification

| Canonical rule / root cause | Producers and consumers covered | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Unknown historical template measurement basis stays unknown | Migration 082, template detail API, order template loader, downstream physical totals | Legacy items remain NULL; `requires_measurement_review` prevents replacement of an existing form with unverified values; import regression covers both verified and legacy templates. |
| Warehouse projections use the same active receipt ledger without an unnecessary nested order/item aggregate | `CargoMetricsService` shared receipt projection, warehouse API/count/search/pagination, status/quantity display and warehouse exports | Warehouse, receiving, cargo, filter and export regressions; real received/imported items and downloaded workbook agree on actual quantities, CBM and weight. No new stock table or cached cargo totals. |
| List payloads need canonical summaries, not edit-detail graphs | Order API `view=list`, query validation, frontend supplier display, customer/supplier/search filters, pagination and default full-detail/export consumers | Compact/full responses compared after removing nested items; invalid view rejected. Detail/edit/export default contract retained. |
| Stale catalog and consequential administrative edits cannot silently overwrite another operator | Product/supplier detail revisions and locked writes/deletes; product classification/description dependencies; create replay ledger; catalog editors and procurement quick-supplier caller; config/business settings; user roles/departments, permission overrides, visibility and sidebar grants | Competing workers, identical/changed create retries, stale deletes, fault-injected audit rollback; headed two-tab product conflict retains entered values. API contract documented. Last active SuperAdmin cannot be removed/deactivated; malformed role/department/active values rejected. |
| Arrival schedules are integer day sets, including zero | Business-settings validation/UI, shared parser, cron selection using one date basis, locked container revalidation, notification targets and delivery intents | `7,3,1,0` persists through UI reload. Cron's remaining `<1` skip was removed. New service commits dedup marker and notifications together, preventing concurrent duplicate scheduling and partial failures. Concurrent workers, day zero, closed-container exclusion, recipient counts, rollback/retry pass. Headed notification opens its exact container. External delivery is queued only after commit. |
| Staff input and navigation state survive normal interaction | Shared numeric/text focus handling, catalog/settings submission guards, late-response protection, current-user capture during permission saves, explicit list filter persistence and Back/Forward fresh requests | Mouse replacement, keyboard editing and search refinement interaction regression; warehouse search/type/status persistence regression; real reload/Back and aborted stock request recovery retain useful filters. Business-critical config/receiving/API responses are not cached. |

No source historical cargo, accounts, catalog classifications, financial records or customer communications were rewritten. Database migrations were the only source deployment writes. Existing in-progress hardening changes were preserved.

## Measured performance

Local headed-session authenticated requests against the same source `clms` dataset, warmed repeated requests, 50-row pages. These are local observations, not a multi-user production load guarantee.

| Request | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Warehouse stock list | Original count query remained running beyond 90 seconds; bounded reproduction also exceeded a 3,000 ms server deadline | 38, 52, 54 ms; 74,194-byte responses, HTTP 200 |
| Orders list | 628, 641, 656 ms; 1,294,835 bytes | 362, 370, 375, 393 ms; 70,604 bytes; HTTP 200 |

Order list payload decreased 94.5%; median measured latency decreased approximately 42%. Warehouse fix removes the redundant aggregate from the query plan rather than caching business-critical stock. Measurement artifacts: `warehouse-query-baseline.json`, `slow-warehouse-count.sql`, `stabilization-performance-after.json` (warehouse samples only), `orders-list-performance.json` under `artifacts/cargo-demo/`. Early route-discovery 403/404 samples are excluded from these claims.

## Final verification

- **54/54 PHP suites pass** after the last application change. Covers the full existing suite, canonical quantities/capacity, exact/full/sequential/bulk assignment, authorization, state machines, real concurrent workers, import replay, rollback fault injection, reports and CSV/XLSX formula safety. Existing permission suite includes 272 assertions.
- **14/14 JS suites pass**; headed `consolidation_interaction_test.cjs` additionally passes actual pointer/keyboard/modal interaction, late responses, selection retention and shared editing behavior. Isolated `security_http_test.cjs` passes cookie, malformed JSON/path, cross-origin and login/logout CSRF checks.
- **293 files pass syntax checks: 238 PHP and 55 JS/CJS/MJS.** `git diff --check` passes; only repository line-ending conversion warnings remain.
- Image export integration also passes with `mime_content_type` disabled, proving the fileinfo fallback. Real multipart upload returns 201; authorized image access returns 200 and anonymous access 401.
- Local tracking HTTP stub verifies actual transport acceptance/rejection, retry counts, disabled/dry-run behavior, credentials failure and replay persistence. No real tracking endpoint or notification channel was called.
- Headed UI on disposable database: over-receipt rejected; order 2467 receives exactly 100 pieces / 10 cartons / 1 CBM / 100 kg; reload and stock agree. Invalid import rejects 110 against 100 remaining; valid two-item import for 2468 commits 200 pieces / 20 cartons / 2 CBM / 200 kg once under double click. Reopened stock and downloaded Excel agree per item.
- UI-created container 856 `NINGBO-BEY-SEP-19-QA` has one order, one item, 100 pieces, 10 cartons, 1/33 CBM and 100/25,000 kg, leaving 32 CBM / 24,900 kg. Order detail references it. Draft 1430 persists booking `NB-BEY-20260919`; UI finalization persists `finalized`, locks references/cargo and correctly reports tracking **disabled**, not pushed. This disposable fixture is removed during cleanup.
- Populated audit log, customer editor/search, dashboard, tracking error details, administrator settings/user save, and catalog conflict behavior verified. QA screenshot and machine-readable results retained under `artifacts/cargo-demo/stabilization-*`; screenshots are QA, not a resumed demo.
- Legacy tests were adjusted to create isolated valid fixtures and assert current canonical rules (finalization eligibility, exact receipt totals/replays, shared permission policy, declared item identifiers). Assertions were not relaxed to accept invalid production behavior. No CLI test was counted as successful real multipart transport without the separate HTTP check above.

## Remaining limitations / acceptance decision

Cleanup verified: disposable `clms_hardening_20260919` and `clms_restore_20260919` databases removed; the manifest/hash-verified QA upload and the owned temporary administrator session removed; local QA server and tracking stub stopped. Temporary session-discovery metadata removed. Before removing the restored database, all **68 comparable source tables** matched its migrated backup checksums (migration bookkeeping excluded): no source operational test data/config changes leaked. Original orders 739–743 remain present. The private verified backup and non-secret regression evidence are retained. The existing Cherry headed browser remains open on Containers; the demo remains stopped.

**NOT READY.** Source preflight verifies schema, required extensions, receiving uniqueness, approved accounting schema and backup rehearsal, but historical cargo integrity, five active seed-password accounts and item-number conflicts remain blocked. The exact records and decisions are listed above as **REQUIRES BUSINESS INPUT**.

Classification and translation review remain incomplete. Real remote tracking/notification acceptance, TLS/provider behavior and cross-instance exactly-once delivery are not proven by a local stub. Off-host immutable audit retention, deployment-wide recovery and a business-approved abandoned-upload retention policy remain unverified. Live offset pagination can change membership between requests; each mutation revalidates current data and exports use a consistent transaction. Local timings do not replace sustained production load testing. The demo remains stopped.

- Catalog revisions/retries: `backend/services/CatalogRevisionService.php`, `backend/api/handlers/{products,suppliers}.php`, `frontend/js/{products,suppliers,procurement_drafts}.js`.
- Administration/scheduling: `backend/services/{SettingsWriteService,ContainerArrivalNotificationService}.php`, `backend/api/handlers/{config,business-settings,users}.php`, `backend/cron/container_arrival_notifications.php`, `includes/sidebar_permissions.php`, `business_settings.php`, `frontend/js/{admin_config,admin_users}.js`.

- Documentation: `docs/API.md`, `docs/production-hardening.md`, `docs/final-stabilization.md`.

## Release blocker closure register — 2026-09-19

Scope limited to the five open gates. This is classification of remaining blockers, not a repeat audit of passed domains. No historical data, account credentials or retention policy changed. Evidence is a fresh read-only source snapshot in `artifacts/cargo-demo/release-blocker-evidence.json`; passwords/hashes are excluded.

### Consolidated historical reconciliation table

Every affected receipt, order item and template item has one row below; overlapping issues are combined. NULL receipt quantity means an absent item ledger, not zero goods. Header totals and present catalog values cannot prove historical item allocations or piece/carton basis. No row can safely be corrected by guessing. Each row is **REQUIRES BUSINESS INPUT**. Classification and translation decisions are separate below.

### Original decision batch - ANSWERED; current decisions below

1. **D1 — History:** supply signed receipt/item packing evidence for the 654-row register, decide item 55's 168/180 discrepancy, approve the customer item-number/reference map, and authorize the specific correction/reversal for finalized records. Name the reconciliation owner.
2. **D2 — Accounts:** for each ID 3, 4, 5, 6, 11, choose retain/rotate or disable. Confirm current responsibilities/visibility replacements and session revocation; identify a secure credential handover route for retained accounts. Do not send passwords in chat.
3. **D3 — Classification/terms:** name the reviewer, approve category definitions and the terminology table, correct `grg`/`rygergh`, and decide whether the exact test-shaped jobs may be archived/removed. Choose approved manual translation at release or configured provider translation.
4. **D4 — Integrations:** choose live-required vs disabled/manual release scope for tracking and email/WhatsApp/translation. If live-required, supply sandbox endpoint, versioned contract, test recipients and secret-store access; no real-customer send is authorized by this investigation.
5. **D5 — Retention:** approve periods, trigger dates, holds and owners for each category above, plus backup RPO/RTO and session idle/absolute limits. Supply off-host immutability/access/rotation/scheduler/restore evidence or explicitly decide the acceptable release controls.

## Approved decisions and closure implementation - 2026-09-19

This section supersedes the unanswered-decision wording above. Houssein approved D1-D5, then added owner incident visibility and encrypted credential recovery. These are targeted additions to release readiness; passed cargo domains were not reopened except for affected authentication, error reporting, and regression checks.

### Historical reconciliation

The consolidated 654-row table remains the single record inventory. All 654 original snapshots are now in `historical_reconciliation_records`, with `HISTORICAL_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED`. Inserts are idempotent and preserve the first captured values. No ordered/received quantity, measurement, number, money, date or shipment state was reconstructed or changed. Item 55 remains 168 ordered / 180 received. Finalized orders 734-738 remain unchanged. Houssein approves any correction; warehouse/operations supplies receiving/packing evidence, accounting/order administration supplies the signed numbering map. Every actual correction must preserve its before value and audit evidence.

Grandfathering is allowed only when lack of current stock/money/shipment/numbering impact is proven. No blanket exemption was inferred. The 12 unknown-basis template items remain blocked from unsafe reuse; they do not require blocking unrelated new work. Other historical gates remain scoped to their affected records. Source checksum comparison confirms no changes to existing cargo, stock, financial, customer, container or shipment tables.

Source escrow contains zero enrolled credentials: there has been no rotation, reveal or session revocation. The known/default credentials remain a release blocker. Owner recovery must be operational before rotating them once.

### Disabled integrations: approved internal-release procedure

Tracking push is disabled with dry-run protection; the finalization button, explanation and confirmation now reflect the server-reported tracking mode before the operator submits, and container help no longer promises a disabled push; notification channels are dashboard only. No real carrier/tracking/email/WhatsApp request was sent. These providers are **EXTERNAL VERIFICATION REQUIRED**, a post-release integration gate, not a requirement for the internal workflow.

Staff use the existing shipment/container booking reference and packing/export documents, perform the carrier booking/tracking lookup manually, and retain actual carrier evidence against that reference using existing supported attachments or the approved business record process. They must not mark a disabled push as delivered or edit locked financial/cargo facts to simulate a send. For customer contact, use the company's approved manual contact process and record the actual outcome; the application dashboard is an internal notice, not proof of external delivery. A failed/disabled push remains visible in Tracking Push Log. Re-enable providers only after sandbox contract, recipient, authentication, retry/idempotency and failure/audit verification, followed by explicit authorization for real sends.

### Approved retention and infrastructure evidence

| data | approved baseline | owner / actual control |
|---|---|---|
| Cargo/shipment/financial | 10 years | Operations + Accounting; no age-only business deletion introduced |
| Linked evidence/uploads | 10 years with parent | Operations; unknown legacy ownership excludes automatic deletion |
| Audit history | 7 years | System owner; immutable/off-host copy still required |

Every legal/business hold overrides deletion until Houssein's explicit release. Holds have approval/release references. No production purge ran. Dry-run found no eligible dated log files or explicitly expired/marked sessions. Undated legacy logs and unowned old sessions/uploads remain preserved; their ownership/date inventory is a documented retention limitation. Incident records follow the application/security history baseline, and resolution audit follows the audit baseline; automated incident-history expiry is not enabled before off-host preservation/hold verification.

Approved sessions: operational users 8-hour idle / 30-day absolute; privileged roles conservatively include SuperAdmin/ChinaAdmin/LebanonAdmin, 30-minute idle / 12-hour absolute. New sessions carry enforced version/fingerprint and actual database identity metadata; both login paths bind to the authenticated database, and cross-database or missing-binding tracked sessions fail closed; pre-policy sessions are not globally revoked until the approved activation plan enables `CLMS_SESSION_POLICY_ENABLED`. Background owner/notification polling does not extend idle time. Recent authentication protects sensitive writes.

No company off-host destination exists. Proposed destination is a dedicated company-controlled Backblaze B2 bucket, with pre-upload encryption, restricted non-destructive upload credentials, separate Houssein ownership, Object Lock and append-resistant audit copies. **OFF-HOST BACKUP EVIDENCE REQUIRED** remains NOT READY. RPO and RTO are each at most four hours. No B2 credentials, bucket, schedule or provider receipt was fabricated.

Local encrypted SQL rehearsal: 74 tables restored into a disposable schema with zero checksum mismatches in 5.601 seconds; archive tampering/truncation/wrong-recipient tests pass. This does not prove off-host upload/download, whole-installation recovery, production load, or four-hour operational recovery. Public-key streaming backup, uploads-archive mode, authentication-before-restore, target-schema credential checks and retention planning are prepared. Production key custody, upload/config restore evidence, scheduled execution and independent off-host recovery remain required.

### Owner controls and security boundary

See [owner-controls.md](owner-controls.md) for configuration, custody and staff procedures. Owner Operations includes deduplicated incidents, revision-protected resolution, user/action/record/audit navigation, recent users/security events, failed-login and integration counts, and high-visibility Critical/High alerts. Expected validation is not treated as a critical fault. No real alert messages were sent. Incident capture does not commit business transactions; unavailable incident storage falls back to a safe diagnostic reference. Raw exception text/stacks and secret request fields are excluded from persisted context.

Credential recovery uses AES-256-GCM bound to user/hash/key ID, protected keys outside SQL, verified owner allowlisting plus current SuperAdmin status, current password, recent sign-in and an independent replay-protected authenticator factor. A forged owner hash in SQL alone does not bypass the factor. Immutable audit acknowledgement is required before disclosure; failed audit or actor/target revocation blocks recovery. Values clear after 20 seconds/blur/navigation and are absent from normal user-management responses. A reset no longer returns plaintext through its ordinary API/UI.

Targeted tests cover encryption/authentication tampering, wrong actor/target/key, stale authentication, audit failure, password-hash forgery, revocation during an in-flight reveal, factor replay/concurrency/throttling, atomic missing-key failure, secret redaction, incident deduplication, UI double-click/late-response/timed clearing, and stale two-tab resolution. Headed QA has verified actual reveal, timed clearing, failed-audit blocking, recent-auth rejection and re-authentication, and saved/stale incident edits. The local signed audit stub is disposable evidence of the client contract, **not** proof of remote immutability.

Production owner configuration and escrow are not activated. `root@localhost` fails the restricted DB boundary. Windows process-owner inspection returned access denied, so database/application OS key separation is unproven. The source has zero escrow rows and unchanged legacy passwords/session versions. Required activation evidence: Houssein account ownership/emergency access, approved mapping, restricted DB/service identities and ACLs, protected key/factor provisioning, HTTPS, and real append-resistant audit acknowledgement/retention.

### Verification boundary and final gate

Only expected source tables changed: migrations, user metadata, approved translation-job cleanup, and their audit entries; additive control tables contain the preserved historical/review flags. Existing cargo/financial/customer/shipment/container table checksums match the pre-closure snapshot. No historical quantity/number/status/amount correction occurred. No account was disabled, promoted or rotated. No external customer action occurred.

**Final regression PASS (2026-09-20): 59/59 PHP suites, 15/15 JS suites, 316/316 syntax checks (257 PHP, 59 JS), HTTP security and persistent headed critical-workflow/owner checks.** Coverage includes permissions, concurrent actions, idempotency, rollback, import/export and finalized-record protections. The clean full run followed all application and fixture changes. Passing regression does not close the explicit business/infrastructure/security activation gates. **NOT READY; demo remains stopped.**


### Final verification evidence - 2026-09-20

Final application changes include database-bound sessions in both login paths, owner filter/revision validation, and truthful disabled-tracking finalization labels. The first final run exposed a test-only midnight dependency: PHP used Europe/Berlin while MySQL SYSTEM had already entered the next day. The draft-filter fixture now uses its persisted created/expected dates; its 28 checks pass. No production timestamp was changed. Deployment must choose and verify its intended PHP/database/business timezone; no historical timestamp conversion is authorized. The clean full rerun follows this fixture correction.

Headed Chromium used the persistent browser with a separate disposable QA context. Order 2398 received 100 pieces / 10 cartons / 1 CBM / 100 kg; over-receipt returned 400. A double-click created one receipt (2417). Container 841 reopened empty, then held the assigned order in draft 1383 with 32 CBM and 24,900 kg remaining. Finalization persisted, protected the finalized draft, and truthfully recorded disabled tracking (log 158), with no external request. Order details, customer relationships, warehouse quantities, audit history and a real XLSX download were verified. The permanent assignment-selection/repaint browser regression and HTTP security checks passed.

Owner browser checks exercised actual password recovery with a second factor, automatic clearing, failed immutable-audit acknowledgement, stale authentication followed by re-login, two-tab incident conflict, persisted resolution, and direct owner-page/API denial for an ordinary employee. The final database-bound login was repeated through the real form and the owner view reopened successfully; missing-binding sessions were rejected. No revealed secret was printed, captured in screenshots, written to artifacts or sent to a real provider.

Seven-request local latency sample during the full suite: orders median 121.82 ms (max 129.60), warehouse 58.13 ms (max 67.66), owner summary 25.52 ms (max 35.31). These are observations under test load, not a comparable before/after optimization claim. Previous measured stabilization improvements remain documented above; this closure pass did not introduce business-data caching.

Evidence files (ignored QA artifacts): `release-final-headed-evidence.json`, `release-owner-latency.json`, `encrypted-recovery-evidence.json`, `release-translation-deletion-proof.json`, `release-closure-state.json`, `stabilization-all-php.json`, `stabilization-all-js.json`, and `stabilization-syntax.json` under `artifacts/cargo-demo/`.

### QA teardown and remaining activation gates

The disposable QA database was dropped after all final suites completed. The three identified QA/stub PHP servers were stopped. Owned QA-only encryption keys, factor material, generated credentials, protected configuration, isolated session files, session metadata artifact and temporary QA logs were removed. Production configuration was not pointed at the QA setup. The separate QA browser context was closed; the original headed browser and staff container page remain open without resuming the demo. Unowned/shared legacy files were not deleted. Source business checksums remain unchanged; approved additive flags/metadata and audited translation cleanup are the only source mutations.

**REQUIRES BUSINESS INPUT:** source documents and Houssein-approved historical numbering/corrections; glossary/classification decisions for the existing register; named future owners for A3/A5/A6; confirmation that Houssein owns and controls account 1. A4 remains approved without an extra permission grant. No historical actor was reassigned.

**SECURITY ACTIVATION REQUIRED:** verify owner/emergency access, provision protected keys and independent factors with custody/recovery, restricted SQL/service identities and OS ACL separation, HTTPS and a real immutable-audit acknowledgement destination; then perform the approved one-time recoverable rotation and session revocation for accounts 3, 4, 5, 6 and 11. Their known seed credentials are still a blocker. Global legacy-session policy activation remains a planned deployment action, not falsely reported as complete.

**OFF-HOST BACKUP EVIDENCE REQUIRED:** configure the real company B2 bucket and restricted credentials, encryption/key recovery and append-resistant audit storage; demonstrate independent off-host download and isolated full application/data/uploads/config restore. Prove scheduled four-hour RPO/RTO, retention/holds and retained generations. Local SQL/encryption tests do not satisfy those infrastructure claims. Tracking/email/WhatsApp/provider end-to-end validation remains **EXTERNAL VERIFICATION REQUIRED** only before those optional integrations are enabled.
